\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}}~=~{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{A}=\alpha\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{A}=\alpha\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{A}=\alpha\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{A}=\alpha\right\}}]}}~=~\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\prod_{a\in A}Z_{a,\alpha_{a}}\Big]\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{i,j}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{i,j}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{i,j}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{i,j}]}}~=~{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{i}=j\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{i}=j\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{i}=j\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{i}=j\right\}}]}}={\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[Z_{i,j}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[Z_{i,j}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[Z_{i,j}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[Z_{i,j}]}}

Note that for any
A\subseteq[m]
with
\left\lvert A\right\rvert=k\leq t/2
,

\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{k}}{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{a\in A}\bigg\lparen\sum_{j\in\Sigma}Z_{a,j}\bigg\rparen\bigg]=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{a\in A}Z_{a,\alpha_{a}}\bigg]=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{a\in A}Z^{2}_{a,\alpha_{a}}\bigg]\geq 0\,.

Thus, the values
\left\{{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{A,\alpha}]}}\right\}_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{A}}
define a distribution
over
\Sigma^{k}
. We call this the local distribution for
\mathbf{Z}_{A}
, or simply for
A
.
Let
\mu:\Sigma^{m}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
be a
k
-local function for
k\leq t/2
, depending on
M\subseteq[m]
. Then,

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}}~=~\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{M}}\bigg\lparen\mu(\alpha)\cdot\prod_{i\in M}Z_{i,\alpha_{i}}\bigg\rparen\bigg]~=~\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{M}}\mu(\alpha)\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\prod_{i\in M}Z_{i,\alpha_{i}}\Big]~=~\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{M}}\mu(\alpha)\cdot{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{M,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{M,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{M,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{M,\alpha}]}}

That is,
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}}
can be seen as the expected value of the function
\mu
under the local distribution for
M
.

5.4
.

Let
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
be a degree-
t
pseudoexpectation. For
k\leq t/2
, let
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}

be two
k
-local functions on
\Sigma^{m}
, depending on the same set of coordinates
M
, and

\mu_{1}(\alpha)\leq\mu_{2}(\alpha)~~\forall\alpha\in\Sigma^{M}
. Then
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}}\leq{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}_{M}
be the local distribution induced by
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
for
\mathbf{Z}_{M}
. Then

{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}}=\mathchoice{\underset{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mu_{1}(\alpha)\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}[\mu_{1}(\alpha)]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}[\mu_{1}(\alpha)]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}[\mu_{1}(\alpha)]}
, and
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}}=\mathchoice{\underset{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mu_{2}(\alpha)\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}[\mu_{2}(\alpha)]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}[\mu_{2}(\alpha)]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\alpha\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{M}}[\mu_{2}(\alpha)]}
, which implies
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{1}(\mathbf{Z})]}}\leq{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu_{2}(\mathbf{Z})]}}
.

The previous claim allows us to replace any local function inside
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
by another local function that dominates it. We will make extensive use of this fact.

Covariance for SoS solutions

Given two sets
S,T\subseteq[m]
with
|S|,|T|\leq k/4
, we can define the covariance between indicator random variables of
\mathbf{Z}_{S}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{T}
taking values
\alpha
and
\beta
respectively, according to the local distribution over
S\cup T
. This is formalized in the next definition.

5.5
.

Let
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
be a pseudodistribution operator of SoS-degree-
t
, and
S,T
are two sets of
size at most
t/4
, and
\alpha\in\Sigma^{S}
,
\beta\in\Sigma^{T}
, we define the pseudo-covariance and
pseudo-variance,

\displaystyle\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha},\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta})~

\displaystyle=~{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\cdot\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}]}}-{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}}\,{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}]}}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}}~

\displaystyle=~\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha},\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha})

The above definition is extended to pseudo-covariance and pseudo-variance for pairs of sets
S,T
,
as the sum of absolute value of pseudo-covariance for all pairs
\alpha,\beta
:

\displaystyle\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S},\mathbf{Z}_{T})~

\displaystyle=~\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{S},\beta\in\Sigma^{T}}\left\lvert\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha},\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta})\right\rvert

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}}~

\displaystyle=~\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma^{S}}\left\lvert{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}}\right\rvert

We will need the fact that
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}}
is bounded above by 1, since,

{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}]}}~=~\sum_{\alpha}\left\lvert{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}}\right\rvert~=~\sum_{\alpha}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}]}}-{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}}^{2}\right\rparen~\leq~\sum_{\alpha}{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}^{2}]}}~=~\sum_{\alpha}{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}]}}~=~1.

Conditioning SoS solutions.

We will also make use of conditioned pseudoexpectation operators, which may be defined in a way
similar to usual conditioning for true expectation operators, as long as the event we condition on
is local.
The conditioned SoS solution is of a smaller degree but continues to respect the constraints that the original solution respects.

5.6
Conditioned SoS Solution.

Let
F\subseteq\Sigma^{m}
be subset (to be thought of as an event) such that
{\mathbf{1}}_{F}:\Sigma^{m}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
is a
k
-local function. Then for every
t>2k
, we can condition a pseudoexpectation operator of SoS-degree
t
on
F
to obtain a new conditioned pseudoexpectation operator
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\given F\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot\given F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot\given F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot\given F]}}
of SoS-degree
t-2k
, as long as
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}}>0
. The conditioned SoS solution is given by

{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[p(\mathbf{Z})\given F(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[p(\mathbf{Z})\given F(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[p(\mathbf{Z})\given F(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[p(\mathbf{Z})\given F(\mathbf{Z})]}}:=\frac{{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[p(\mathbf{Z})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[p(\mathbf{Z})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[p(\mathbf{Z})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[p(\mathbf{Z})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}}}{{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\mathbf{1}}^{2}_{F}(\mathbf{Z})]}}}

where
p
is any polynomial of degree at most
t-2k
.

We can also define pseudocovariances and pseudo-variances for the conditioned SoS solutions.

5.7
Pseudocovariance.

Let
F\subseteq\Sigma^{m}
be an event such that
{\mathbf{1}}_{F}
is
k
-local, and let
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
be a pseudoexpectation operator of degree
t
, with
t>2k
. Let
S,T
be two sets of size at most
\frac{t-2k}{2}
each. Then the pseudocovariance between
\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}
for the solution conditioned on event
F
is defined as,

\displaystyle\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha},\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F)~=~{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F]}}-{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|F\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|F]}}~{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|F]}}

We also define the pseudocovariance between
\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}
after
conditioning on a random assignment for some
\mathbf{Z}_{V}
with
V\subseteq[m]
.
Note that here the random assignment for
\mathbf{Z}_{V}
is chosen according to the local distribution for
the set
V
.

5.8
Pseudocovariance for conditioned pseudoexpectation operators.

\displaystyle\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha},\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|\mathbf{Z}_{V})~=~\sum_{\gamma\in\Sigma^{V}}\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha},\mathbf{Z}_{T,\beta}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}=\gamma)\cdot{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{V,\gamma}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{V,\gamma}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{V,\gamma}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{V,\gamma}]}}

And we likewise define
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S,\alpha}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}]}}
,
\tildecov(\mathbf{Z}_{S},\mathbf{Z}_{T}|\mathbf{Z}_{V})
and
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{S}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}[\mathbf{Z}_{S}|\mathbf{Z}_{V}]}}
.

5.1
SoS relaxations for codes

Pseudocodewords for AEL

Let
G(L,R,E)
be the bipartite
(n,d,\lambda)
-expander on which the AEL code is defined, and let
\mathcal{C}=(\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
be the inner code. The SoS variables will be
\mathbf{Z}=\{Z_{e,j}\}_{e\in E,j\in\Sigma}
, where
\Sigma={\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{b_{\mathrm{in}}}
.

The subsets of
E
we consider will all be vertex neighborhoods from the left or right. To simplify notation, we use the convention that if
\ell\in L
, then
\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}
denotes
\mathbf{Z}_{N(\ell)}
, where
N(\ell)
is the set of all edges incident on the left vertex
\ell
. Likewise, if
r\in R
,
\mathbf{Z}_{r}
denotes
\mathbf{Z}_{N(r)}
, where
N(r)
denotes the set of all edges incident on the right vertex
r
.

We also extend this shorthand to include subsets of
L
and
R
so that if
S\subseteq L
(
R
respectively), then
\mathbf{Z}_{S}
denotes
\mathbf{Z}_{N(S)}
where
N(S)
is the set of all edges in
E
incident on left (right respectively) vertices in
S
.

5.9
X
-Pseudocodewords.

For
t\geq 2d
, we define a degree-
t
AEL pseuocodeword to be a degree-
t
pseudoexpectation operator
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
on
\mathbf{Z}
respecting the following constraints:

\displaystyle\forall\,\ell\in L,\quad\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}

We now define a generalized version of the notion of distance between a pseudocodeword
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
, and a true codeword
h
.

5.10

We define three types of distances between a pseudocodeword
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
of SoS-degree
t\geq 2d
and a (unfolded) codeword
h\in\Sigma^{E}
are defined as

\displaystyle\Delta_{L}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen~

\displaystyle=~\mathchoice{\underset{\ell\sim L}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell\sim L}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell\sim L}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell\sim L}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\neq h_{\ell}\right\}}]}}]}

Next, we define a non-convex property for a pseudocodeword that allows us to prove a distance property similar to
Lemma

4.1
for pseudocodewords.

5.11
.

A pseudocodeword is
\eta
-good if,

\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\tildecov}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}\right]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[{\mathchoice{{\tildecov}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}\right]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[{\mathchoice{{\tildecov}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}\right]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[{\mathchoice{{\tildecov}\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}\right]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}{{\tildecov}[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}]}}]}\leq\eta

The following lemma from
[
JST23
]
captures the main property of
\eta
-good pseudocodoewords that we will be using. The proof of this lemma can be found in
Appendix

A
where it is restated as
Lemma

A.4
.

5.12
.

Let
\{X_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}
and

be two collections of

-local functions on
\Sigma^{E}
such that for every

\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[X_{\ell}(\mathbf{Z})\cdot Y_{r}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[X_{\ell}(\mathbf{Z})\cdot Y_{r}(\mathbf{Z})]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[X_{\ell}(\mathbf{Z})\cdot Y_{r}(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[X_{\ell}(\mathbf{Z})\cdot X_{\ell}\rVert_{\infty}\Big\rparen\Big\lparen\max_{r}\lVert Y_{r}\rVert_{\infty}\Big\rparen

Next, we show that a variant of the expander mixing lemma can be adapted to work inside the SoS proof system by exploiting the non-negativity of sum-of-squares of low-degree polynomials. The next lemma is again borrowed from
[
JST23
]
, although it has appeared in different forms before in the literature, and we include a proof in

where it is restated as

.

5.13
EML for pseudoexpectations.

Let
\{X_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}
and

-local functions on

6
SoS Proof of Distance

We start by extending the definition of partial minimizer to be a vector-valued local function so that can be used inside pseudoexpectations. For a fixed codeword
h
, we define

\psi(\mathbf{Z},h)_{\ell}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\;\;\text{ if }\;\;\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\not\in\,h_{\ell}+\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}\\[6.0pt]
h_{\ell}\;\;\text{ if }\;\;\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\in\,h_{\ell}+\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}\end{cases}

More explicitly,
\psi(\mathbf{Z},h)_{\ell}
is shorthand for
{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\not\in h_{\ell}+\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}\right\}}\cdot\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}+{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}\in h_{\ell}+\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}\right\}}\cdot h_{\ell}
.

(
Coset-preserving)

\displaystyle\psi(\mathbf{Z},h)_{\ell}\in\,\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}+\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}\iff{\varphi}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{{X}\mathstrut}}^{\raisebox{0.90417pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-1$}}(\psi(\mathbf{Z},h)_{\ell})={\varphi}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{{X}\mathstrut}}^{\raisebox{0.90417pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-1$}}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})\;\;\forall\,l\in L

(4)

(
Monotone)

\displaystyle{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\psi(\mathbf{Z},h)_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}~\leq~{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\;\;\forall\,r\in\,R

(5)

Recall that
{\varphi}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{{X}\mathstrut}}^{\raisebox{0.90417pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-1$}}
is the local inversion map defined in
Definition

3.4
. We emphasize that the partial minimizer is importantly defined in a way that the monotonicity property is true
locally
.

Next, we prove a distance bound analogous to
Lemma

4.1
for pseudocodewords that satisfy the
\eta
-good property from
Definition

5.11
. This is the key statement we need to make the framework from
[
JST23
]
applicable to the quantum AEL setting.

6.1
Distance proof.

Let
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
be an
\eta
-good pseudocodeword and
h\in\Sigma^{E}
be such that
\fold(h)
is a codeword in
\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
and
\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen>0
. Then,

\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen~\geq~\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda+\eta}{\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen}\,.

h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}+\lambda+\eta

(
Eq.

5
)

\displaystyle=~\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen\cdot\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen+\lambda+\eta\;.

Comparing the two sides, we get

\displaystyle\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen\cdot\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen+\lambda+\eta~\geq~\delta_{\mathrm{in}}\cdot\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{\cdot}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\cdot}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\cdot}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\cdot}]}}\,,h\right\rparen\;.

Dividing by
\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{\cdot}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\cdot}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\cdot}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{\cdot}]}}\,,h\right\rparen
gives us the result.

7
List Decoding Algorithm

In this section, we show how to use the machinery of
[
JST23
]
to develop a list decoding algorithm for the quantum codes obtained by AEL amplification. The two main components of this machinery are the (algorithmic) covering lemma and the (SoS) distance proof. The former lets us discover an object "containing" the list, while the latter allows the list elements to be extracted from this object.

We start with a subsection on covering lemmas (
Section

7.1
), which we then combine with the SoS distance proof from
Section

6
in
Section

7.2
. We show how to derandomize the algorithm in
Section

7.3
, followed by the application to QLDPC codes in
Section

7.4
.

We stress that all the results in this section follow the outline from
[
JST23
]
, except with minor changes to accommodate the differences in the quantum setting, such as the change from
\Delta_{L}(\cdot,\cdot)
to
\Delta_{L,\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}}(\cdot,\cdot)
. We choose to write down the formal details due to these minor changes, but defer some proofs to the appendix if they are exactly same as in the classical case.

7.1
Covering Lemma

As we saw in
Section

5
, pseudocodewords can be seen as a relaxation of the set of distributions over codewords. For list decoding, we would like to ensure that a (pseudo-)distribution does not completely ignore some of the codewords in the list. In particular, we would like a way to avoid point distributions over single codewords.

The key idea in
[
JST23
]
, going back further to
[
AJQ+20
]
, is that maximizing certain entropy measure indeed allows us to obtain a (pseudo-)distribution that is close to
every
codeword in the list. We slightly modify the statements from
[
JST23
]
to focus only on the large alphabet case. This part of the algorithm is very analytic in that it works on embeddings of codewords into Euclidean spaces, and does not need to care about the underlying code structure.

In particular, there is no difference between classical and quantum codes in the embedding and covering lemma, but the proof of Johnson bound using the covering lemma takes into account the different notion of distance, so that we have distance only between pairs of codewords in
\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
that belong to different cosets of
\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
.

For any
f\in\Sigma^{E}
, recall that
f_{r}\in\Sigma^{d}
is the restriction of
f
to the edge-neighborhood
N(r)
of a vertex
r\in R
. Let us denote the size of alphabet
\Sigma={\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{b_{\mathrm{in}}}
by
s:=q^{b_{\mathrm{in}}}
.

7.1
Embedding.

Let
{\mathbb{R}}^{s^{d}}
be spanned by formal orthonormal basis vectors
\{e_{w}\,\mid\,w\in\Sigma^{d}\}
. Define an embedding
\chi:\Sigma^{E}\rightarrow\lparen{\mathbb{R}}^{s^{d}}\rparen^{R}\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{s^{d}\cdot n}
, as
\chi(f)_{r}=e_{f_{r}}
for each
r\in R
. We denote the image of a subspace,
U
under this map
\chi(U)
. The Euclidean inner product for this embedding captures the
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen\cdot\,,\cdot\right\rparen
distance as,

\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f_{1}\,,f_{2}\right\rparen=1-\left\langle\chi(f_{1}),\chi(f_{2})\right\rangle.

We will use this embedding to prove the following lemma, as well as its algorithmic version
Lemma

7.4
. Since the next lemma is only used to prove the existential Johnson bound, and is completely same as the classical case in
[
JST23
]
, we defer its proof to
Appendix

C
.

7.2
Covering Lemma.

Let
g\in\Sigma^{E}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
There exists a distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
over
\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
with support size at most
s^{d}\cdot n+1
such that for any
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
such that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha
, the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
satisfies

\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}<1-\alpha^{2}\,.

Using the covering lemma above, we now prove the Johnson bound for quantum CSS codes obtained by AEL amplification. While this proof can be easily adapted for general CSS codes, we choose to write it for the specific case of quantum AEL codes of
[
BGG24
]
in preparation for its algorithmic version in
Lemma

7.4
.

7.3
Alphabet-free Johnson bound.

Let
\mathcal{F}=(\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
be a CSS code of distance
\delta
obtained by AEL amplification, so that
\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}=\fold(\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}})
and
\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}=\fold(\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
. Then for any
g\in\Sigma^{E}
,

\lvert{\mathcal{L}}(g,1-\sqrt{1-\delta})\rvert~\leq~s^{d}\cdot n+1

Proof.

We use
Lemma

7.2
for
g
to obtain a distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
over
\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
of support size at most
s^{d}\cdot n+1
. This distribution has the property that for any
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
such that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\sqrt{1-\delta}
, it holds that

\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}~<~\delta

(6)

Fix a coset (of
\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
) in the list
{\mathcal{L}}(g,1-\sqrt{1-\delta})
and let
h
be the codeword from this coset that is nearest to
g
in
\Delta_{R}(\cdot,\cdot)
metric, so that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\sqrt{1-\delta}
. We claim that the support of
{\mathcal{D}}
must contain a member of the coset
h+\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
. If not, every
f\in\supp({\mathcal{D}})
satisfies
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen\geq\delta
due to the distance of the code
\mathcal{F}
, and so,

\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}~\geq~\delta

which contradicts
Eq.

6
. Since the total support of
{\mathcal{D}}
is bounded by
s^{d}\cdot n+1
, the number of cosets in the list is also bounded by
s^{d}\cdot n+1
.

While the covering lemma is sufficient to conclude an upper bound on the list size up to the Johnson bound, it is not clear how to
efficiently
find the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
promised in the covering lemma. However, that distribution is shown to exist as the optimizer of certain convex function over the set of distributions over codewords. The next lemma shows that optimizing for the same convex function over
pseudocodewords
also gives similar covering properties, but has the advantage that such an optimal degree-
t
pseudocodeword can be found via SDPs in time
n^{{\mathcal{O}}(t)}
.

7.4
Algorithmic Covering Lemma.

Let
g\in\Sigma^{E}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1-\alpha)
.
There exists a pseudocodeword
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
of degree
t\geq d
such that for every
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
such that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha-\varepsilon
, it holds that

\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha^{2}-2\alpha\varepsilon\,.

Moreover, such a pseudocodeword can be found in time
n^{{\mathcal{O}}(t)}
.

Proof.

We claim that a pseudocodeword of degree
t
that minimizes
\lVert{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\rVert^{2}=\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\right\rangle
among all degree-
t
pseudocodewords that satisfy
\Delta_{R}({\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}},g)<1-\alpha-\varepsilon
has the required property above.
We need to argue that for every
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
such that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha-\varepsilon
, it holds that

\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},\chi(h)\right\rangle>\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon\,.

(7)

This suffices to prove the lemma as

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\underset{r\in R}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}\neq h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\underset{r\in R}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[1-{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}

\displaystyle=1-\mathchoice{\underset{r\in R}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{r}=h_{r}\right\}}]}}]}

\displaystyle=1-\mathchoice{\underset{r\in R}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in R}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{Z}_{r}),\chi(h_{r})\right\rangle]}}]}

\displaystyle=1-\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},\chi(h)\right\rangle

We also note the following lower bound on
\Psi\Big\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\Big\rparen
:

\displaystyle\big\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\,,\,{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\big\rangle\cdot\left\langle\chi(g),\chi(g)\right\rangle~\geq~\big\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\,,\,\chi(g)\big\rangle^{2}~>~(\alpha+\varepsilon)^{2}

(8)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\Psi\Big\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\Big\rparen~=~\big\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\,,\,{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\big\rangle~>~(\alpha+\varepsilon)^{2}~>~\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon

(9)

Suppose
Eq.

7
does not hold. Then there exists an
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
such that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha-\varepsilon
(which is equivalent to
\left\langle\chi(g),\chi(h)\right\rangle>\alpha+\varepsilon
), but

\displaystyle\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},\chi(h)\right\rangle\leq\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon\,.

For a
{\theta}\in[0,1]
to be chosen later, consider the degree-
t
pseudocodeword
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}}
which can be evaluated for any (vector-valued)
t
-local function
\mu
as

{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\mu(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}}=(1-{\theta})\cdot{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mu(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\mu(\mathbf{Z})]}}+{\theta}\cdot\mu(h)\,.

We will reach a contradiction to the optimality of
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
by showing that
\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen<\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen
.

Since every integral codeword like
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
is also a degree-
t
pseucodeword, this convex combination is also a degree-
t
pseudocodeword, and it is also easy to see that
\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},\chi(g)\right\rangle~>~\alpha+\varepsilon
.

\displaystyle\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen

\displaystyle=\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=~\left\langle(1-{\theta})\cdot{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}+{\theta}\cdot\chi(h),(1-{\theta})\cdot{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}+{\theta}\cdot\chi(h)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=~(1-{\theta})^{2}\cdot\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}}\right\rangle+2\cdot{\theta}(1-{\theta})\cdot\left\langle{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\chi(\mathbf{Z})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\chi(\mathbf{Z})]}},\chi(h)\right\rangle+{\theta}^{2}\cdot\left\langle\chi(h),\chi(h)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq~(1-{\theta})^{2}\cdot\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen+2\cdot{\theta}(1-{\theta})\cdot\lparen\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon\rparen+{\theta}^{2}\;.

The optimal
{\theta}
is given by

{\theta}^{*}~=~\frac{\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen-\lparen\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon\rparen}{\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen-2\lparen\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon\rparen+1}

Since
\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen>\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\varepsilon
from
Eq.

9
, we get optimal
{\theta}^{*}>0
, which implies
\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen<\Psi\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\right\rparen
.

7.2
Putting things together

Next, we combine the algorithmic covering lemma
Lemma

7.4
and the distance proof
Lemma

6.1
to give a list decoding algorithm. However, note that the distance proof is only applicable to
\eta
-good pseudocodewords, and the pseudocodeword found by
Lemma

7.4
need not have this property.

To remedy this, we start with a lemma that says that the
\eta
-good property can be obtained by random conditioning. This technique is common in algorithmic applications of Sum-of-Squares, and first appeared in
[
BRS11
]
. It was adapted for decoding in
[
JST23
]
. Since the proof is exactly the same as in
[
JST23
]
, we defer the proof to
Appendix

A
.

7.5
Correlation Rounding.

Let
\eta>0
, and
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
be a pseudocodeword of degree at least
2d\left(\frac{s^{3d}}{\eta^{2}}+1\right)
. Then there exists an integer
u^{*}\leq s^{3d}/\eta^{2}
such that

\mathchoice{\underset{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}]}\leq\eta\,.

where
r_{1},r_{2},\cdots r_{u^{*}}
are independent randomly chosen vertices of
R
.

That is, conditioning on the neighborhoods of
u^{*}
many randomly chosen vertices in
R
renders the pseudocodeword
\eta
-good in expectation. This can be seen as a form of "sampling" from the pseudodistribution. Sampling from a true distribution of codewords can be seen as conditioning on all
n
vertex neighborhoods in
R
, and a sampled codeword is always integral, which implies it is
\eta
-good with
\eta=0
.
Lemma

7.5
shows that there is some non-convex property we can derive even with just constantly many conditionings, and that this argument is also valid for pseudodistributions that do not have high enough moments to support sampling.

We now get to the main technical theorem of this work that gives an algorithm to list decode codes constructed via quantum AEL amplification of unique decodable base codes. We first present a randomized version that outputs each codeword in the list
{\mathcal{L}}
with some small but constant probability. We can repeat this algorithm to output a list of cosets that contains the true list of cosets with probability arbitrarily close to 1. In the next subsection, we will derandomize this algorithm, but will end up with much bigger list sizes since pruning the list can be hard for quantum codes.

7.6
List Decoding AEL amplification.

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
be the code obtained by applying quantum AEL amplification to the outer code
(\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
and inner code
(\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
of distance
\delta_{\mathrm{in}}
using an
(n,d,\lambda)
–expander graph. The inner code
(\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{C}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
is over an alphabet
\Sigma
of size
s
, so that
(\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
is over alphabet
\Sigma^{d}
of size
s^{d}
.

Suppose the code
(\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
can be unique-decoded from radius
\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}
in time
{\mathcal{T}}(n)
. Assume that
\lambda<\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}
.

Then for any
\gamma>0
, there exists an algorithm based on
s^{{\mathcal{O}}(d)}/\varepsilon^{4}
levels of the SoS hierarchy that given
g\in\Sigma^{E}
, runs in time
\ln(1/\gamma)\cdot\left[n^{s^{{\mathcal{O}}(d)}/\varepsilon^{4}}+{\mathcal{T}}(n)\right]
and produces a list
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
of cosets that contains the list of cosets
{\mathcal{L}}:={\mathcal{L}}\left\lparen g,{\mathcal{J}}\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}\right\rparen-\varepsilon\right\rparen
with probability at least
1-\gamma
. The size of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
is at most
\widetilde{\Omega}_{s,d}\left\lparen\frac{\ln(1/\gamma)}{\varepsilon^{6}}\right\rparen
.

Proof.

The decoding algorithm is presented as
Algorithm

7.7
.
Recall that
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}\left\lparen g,{\mathcal{J}}\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}\right\rparen-\varepsilon\right\rparen
is the list of all cosets in
\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}/\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
that intersect the Hamming ball
{\mathcal{B}}\left\lparen g,{\mathcal{J}}\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}\right\rparen-\varepsilon\right\rparen
.

Algorithm 7.7

(List Decoding)
.

Input

g\in\Sigma^{E}
,
\gamma\in(0,1)

Output

List of cosets
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}/\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
that contains
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}\left\lparen g,{\mathcal{J}}\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}\right\rparen-\varepsilon\right\rparen

•

Pick
\eta=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}{16\delta_{\mathrm{in}}}
and
M=\frac{\ln(1/\gamma)}{p\ln p}
, where
p=\frac{\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}\right\rparen^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{6}}{2^{12}\cdot s^{3d}\cdot\delta_{\mathrm{in}}^{4}}
.

•

Use
Lemma

7.4
to obtain a pseudocodeword of degree
t\geq 2d(\frac{s^{3d}}{\eta^{2}}+1)
such that for every
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
which satisfies
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<{\mathcal{J}}\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}\right\rparen-\varepsilon
, it holds that

\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen~<~\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}-\varepsilon

•

Initialize
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}=\emptyset
.

•

Repeat
M
times:

(i)

Choose
u
uniformly at random from
\{1,2,\cdots,\frac{s^{3d}}{\eta^{2}}\}
.

(ii)

Choose a random subset
U\subseteq R
of size
u
, and let
N(U)
denote the edge neighborhood of
U
. Sample a random assignment
\sigma
for
N(U)
using the local distribution for this set of edges. That is,
\sigma
is chosen with probability
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma\right\}}]}}
.

(iii)

Condition
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
on
\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma
, and let
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\cdot]}}={\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\given\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot\given\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot\given\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot\given\mathbf{Z}_{U}=\sigma]}}
be the conditioned pseudocodeword of degree
2d\left\lparen\frac{s^{3d}}{\eta^{2}}+1\right\rparen-2\cdot d\cdot u\geq 2d
.

(iv)

Generate
y\in({\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{b_{\mathrm{out}}})^{L}
by independently sampling
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})]}}
. That is, for
w\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{b_{\mathrm{out}}}
, the probability that
y_{\ell}=w
is
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})=w\right\}}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})=w\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})=w\right\}}]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{*}}[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{\varphi_{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\ell})=w\right\}}]}}
.

(v)

Call the
X
-decoder of
(\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}},\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}})
on
y
. If a coset
\overline{z}+\mathcal{D}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
is found close to
y
,
then add to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
the coset
\widetilde{{\varphi}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{{X}\mathstrut}}}(\overline{z})+\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
.

•

Prune
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
to only include one representative per coset, via Gaussian elimination.

•

Return
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
.

We now argue that the probability of all the cosets in
{\mathcal{L}}
being included in
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
is at least
1-\gamma
. Fix such a coset in
{\mathcal{L}}
and let
h\in\Sigma^{E}
be the nearest codeword to
g
from this coset
h+\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{Z\mathstrut}}^{\perp}
, so that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<{\mathcal{J}}\left\lparen\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}\right\rparen-\varepsilon
.
Lemma

7.4
implies that
{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}\left\lparen{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[\cdot]}}\,,h\right\rparen~<~\delta_{\mathrm{in}}-\frac{\lambda}{\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}-\varepsilon\;.

(10)

To be able to use the distance proof of
Lemma

6.1
, we need the pseudocodeword to be
\eta
-good.
Lemma

7.5
shows that this can be obtained by random conditioning. In particular, there exists a
u^{*}\in\{1,2,\cdots,\frac{s^{3d}}{\eta^{2}}\}
such that,

\mathchoice{\underset{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{r_{1},r_{2},\cdots,r_{u^{*}}}[\mathchoice{\underset{\ell,r}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell,r}[\tildecov\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\ell},\mathbf{Z}_{r}|\mathbf{Z}_{r_{1}},\mathbf{Z}_{r_{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{Z}_{r_{u^{*}}}\right]]}]}~\leq~\eta\;.

(11)

Note that we picked
\eta=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{\mathrm{dec}}}{16\delta_{\mathrm{in}}}
in
Algorithm

7.7
, so that
u^{*}
is bounded by a constant independent of
n
.

Suppose

is chosen in step (i) of

to be

, which happens with probability at least

. Rewriting

to denote the set of all edges incident on

, we get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}U\subseteq R\\
