\displaystyle~=~\mathchoice{\underset{i,j}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{i,j}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{i,j}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{i,j}[{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mathbf{Z}))(x_{j}X_{j}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{j}Y_{j}(\mathbf{Z}))]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}[(x_{i}X_{i}(\mathbf{Z})+y_{i}Y_{i}(\mat By Cholesky decomposition, there exist vectors

to these vectors.

Appendix C
Covering Lemma

C.1
Covering Lemma.

Let
g\in\Sigma^{E}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
There exists a distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
over
\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
with support size at most
s^{d}\cdot n+1
such that for any
h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
such that
\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha
, the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
satisfies

\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}<1-\alpha^{2}\,.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}=\left\{h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}{\;\;:\;\;}\Delta_{R}\left\lparen g\,,h\right\rparen<1-\alpha\right\}=\left\{h\in\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}{\;\;:\;\;}\left\langle\chi(g),\chi(h)\right\rangle>\alpha\right\}
.

Define the set
T=\left\{v\in\mathrm{conv}\left\lparen\chi(\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}})\right\rparen{\;\;:\;\;}\left\langle v,\chi(g)\right\rangle>\alpha\right\}
.

C.2
.

Let
g_{0}=\argmin_{v\in T}\lVert v\rVert^{2}
. Then for any
h\in{\mathcal{H}}
, it holds that
\left\langle g_{0},\chi(h)\right\rangle>\alpha^{2}
.

To finish the proof using this claim, we write
g_{0}
as a convex combination of elements in
\chi(\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}})
and let the corresponding distribution over
\mathcal{E}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}}
be
{\mathcal{D}}
. By the Carathéodory theorem,
{\mathcal{D}}
can be chosen with support size at most
s^{d}\cdot n+1
. Finally,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\Delta_{R}\left\lparen f\,,h\right\rparen]}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[1-\left\langle\chi(f),\chi(h)\right\rangle\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[1-\left\langle\chi(f),\chi(h)\right\rangle]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[1-\left\langle\chi(f),\chi(h)\right\rangle]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[1-\left\langle\chi(f),\chi(h)\right\rangle]}

\displaystyle=1-\left\langle\mathchoice{\underset{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\chi(f)\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\chi(f)]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\chi(f)]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{f\sim{\mathcal{D}}}[\chi(f)]},\chi(h)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=1-\left\langle g_{0},\chi(h)\right\rangle<1-\alpha^{2}\hfill\qquad\hbox{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}

We next prove the claim.

Proof of
C.2
.

Note that
\lVert g_{0}\rVert\cdot\lVert\chi(g)\rVert\geq\left\langle g_{0},\chi(g)\right\rangle>\alpha
means
\lVert g_{0}\rVert>\alpha
.

If not, there exists an
h\in{\mathcal{H}}
such that
\left\langle g_{0},\chi(h)\right\rangle\leq\alpha^{2}
. For a
{\theta}\in[0,1]
to be chosen later, consider
g_{1}={\theta}\cdot g_{0}+(1-{\theta})\cdot\chi(h)
, which is also in
\mathrm{conv}\left\lparen\chi(\mathcal{F}_{\lx@scalerel@obj{X\mathstrut}})\right\rparen
, and
\left\langle g_{1},\chi(g)\right\rangle={\theta}\cdot\left\langle g_{0},\chi(g)\right\rangle+(1-{\theta})\cdot\left\langle\chi(h),\chi(g)\right\rangle>\alpha
.

\displaystyle\lVert g_{1}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle~=~\left\langle{\theta}\cdot g_{0}+(1-{\theta})\cdot\chi(h),{\theta}\cdot g_{0}+(1-{\theta})\cdot\chi(h)\right\rangle

\displaystyle~=~{\theta}^{2}\cdot\lVert g_{0}\rVert^{2}+2{\theta}(1-{\theta})\cdot\left\langle g_{0},\chi(h)\right\rangle+(1-{\theta})^{2}\cdot\lVert\chi(h)\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle~\leq~{\theta}^{2}\cdot\lVert g_{0}\rVert^{2}+2{\theta}(1-{\theta})\cdot\alpha^{2}+(1-{\theta})^{2}

The optimum of this quadratic function is strictly less than
\lVert g_{0}\rVert^{2}
if
\lVert g_{0}\rVert>\alpha
, which is indeed the case as
\lVert g_{0}\rVert\cdot\lVert\chi(g)\rVert\geq\left\langle g_{0},\chi(g)\right\rangle>\alpha
means
\lVert g_{0}\rVert>\alpha
.
